However, neither of them had been able to finish her sentence. The Italian girl’s fist had clenched Maya’s gown - at her stomach - and she’d pulled her toward her, her other arm wrapping around her neck, while Maya’s arms wrapped around her waist, and they’d kissed. 

The blonde lifted the brunette off the ground - even though she was already taller than her to begin with - and even more so in her heels. They kissed for several long seconds before pulling their lips apart, huge smiles on their faces. 

- “Your speech was epic, baby.”

- “Did you realize I was talking about you? When I mentioned soulmates and those who’ve already met the person they want to spend their life with.”

- “I was hoping you were talking about me.”

- “I wasn’t talking about anyone else. I have no idea who in this graduating class has met the love of their life. All I know is that I’ve met mine. My best friend. My lover. My life. My heart. My one and only.”

- “I love you so much, Carina DeLuca. I can’t wait to live on campus with you.”

- “You know we’re probably going to miss our freshman year…” the brunette asked, smiling against her lips. 

- “Why’s that?”

- “Bambina… Living with you? I’m going to want to make love… All the time.”

- “In that case, we’re going to stay in college for a very long time...”

- “Why’s that?” Carina asked, furrowing her brow but still smiling.

- “Because I’m not suddenly going to stop wanting you in a year,” replied the American, sliding one hand up her girlfriend’s back and kissing her again. 

- “Can we congratulate our graduates?” asked Lucia as she approached with Lane and Katherine. 

- “Yes, of course,” Carina replied with a huge smile on her face as Maya set her down on the ground. 

-  “That was a beautiful speech.”

Lane had walked over to Carina and hugged her briefly but sincerely.

Maya remained silent.

Yet that simple gesture had stirred something warm in her chest. She knew what that embrace meant. Above all, she knew everything that lay behind it.

She’d been so afraid that their relationship would change the way Lane looked at her.

And finally, there he was. Congratulating them. Accepting Carina. Accepting their relationship.

Maya exchanged a glance with her girlfriend, unable to hold back her smile.

They didn’t need words. 

The parents had congratulated the two young women with a hug each, then the blonde had slipped her hand into the Italian girl’s to interlace their fingers. Victoria had called them over for the endless photo s with friends, but the American had signaled to her to say, «Just two minutes.»

- “Lane’s right. What a speech, my daughter!” said Lucia. 

“I’m so proud of both of you. And next year… UCLA, so you can both live out your dreams… It’s… impressive.”

- “I couldn’t see myself being away from Maya for the next four years. We both needed to be able to thrive… while staying together,” Carina replied, looking at her girlfriend. 

- “After all… As you so aptly put it… Some of you have already met your soulmate…” Lane added, smiling sincerely.

They both had huge smiles on their faces. At the beginning of their relationship, they wouldn’t have thought Lane would support them… But maybe it was because of the heated discussion between Carina and Vincenzo. Or maybe because he’d seen that their relationship wasn’t affecting Maya’s athletic career. But he’d eventually come around. Much to the delight of the two young women and their mother.

They’d talked about how eager they were to live on the UCLA campus. They’d even asked to share a room. They were already talking about the sororities they’d like to join in their sophomore year. 

But they knew they weren’t there yet. Before UCLA, there was summer, vacation - as Carina had so aptly put it in her speech. 

Before they left to join their friends, Katherine gently rested her hand on her daughter’s forearm. 

- “So… Is everything ready for your road trip to Sicily? You’re leaving soon!”

Then Maya and Carina looked at each other tenderly. Their smiles widened.

In love.

Because yes, before heading to the Los Angeles campus, they were going - just as they’d planned one December morning - to spend a month touring Sicily.

That little island where Carina was born. And which, without knowing it, would soon welcome the place that had become her home. Her new home.

Maya gently clasped her hand in hers.

They still had everything to discover. A new city. A new life. A new adventure. Hundreds of memories they hadn’t yet made.

But for the first time, they weren’t looking toward the future with apprehension.

They were looking at it together.

And perhaps that was, after all, the most beautiful thing they had gained over the past few years.

They had started high school as two strangers who thought they knew exactly where their lives would take them. 

They had met and become friends. Then best friends. And today they were leaving high school as a couple, knowing only one thing for certain.

They would move forward side by side. As for their story, it was just beginning.
